- E at position 2
- Y (or F) at position 4

- D/N at position 7 (second calcium ligand)

The question is whether the first hydrophobic segment is a signal peptide or a TM helix.

Characteristics of signal peptides:
- Usually 15-30 residues
- Have a hydrophobic core
- Often followed by a short polar region before the mature protein starts
- Cleaved at a specific site

Characteristics of first TM helix:
- Usually preceded by a short N-terminal region (a few residues)

- Can have single or multiple transmembrane domains

**Plasma membrane:**
- Typically has signal peptide + multiple domains
- Often has N-terminal signal sequences that are cleaved

**Mitochondrial outer membrane:**
- Known for having a single C-terminal or N-terminal transmembrane segment (TAIL anchor proteins have C-terminal)

- The combination of a single short hydrophobic segment followed by a large hydrophilic domain is characteristic of mitochondrial outer membrane proteins, particularly those with beta-barrel structures or single-pass transmembrane proteins

The key distinguishing features for mitochondrial outer membrane:
1. Single transmembrane segment
2. No ER signal sequence

The absence of a mitochondrial targeting presequence (which would be needed for matrix localization) and the presence of a single hydrophobic anchor followed by a large hydrophilic domain is the hallmark of outer membrane proteins.
